And there is a limit of merit, namely: One can earn € 10,000 in the year without losing the family allowance. There are, of course, occasional situations where this limit of merit is slightly exceeded. You don't know that exactly, you can't plan it exactly and correctly, and suddenly it can happen that you get a notification from the Ministry of Finance, maybe six months later, where it says that the family aid can be repaid for the whole of the past year. This can be several thousand euros, and this can lead to a student who does not necessarily have a high savings rate, but that is suddenly a threat to existence for him. So the crossing of this border by only a few euros can lead to 100 percent repayment of the family aid. And that's what we're changing now! We are now making a grinding arrangement. In the last few years, a total of 1,662 people have been affected who have fallen into this “fail” – under quotation marks. And from now on, there's a grinding control. This applies in the calendar year following the 19th century. The birthday of the person concerned, but also for this calendar year, for this year. This grinding system provides for my knowledge that the part you have earned too much will have to be paid back, but in any case not everything. I mean, that's very important. We talked about this in the Family Committee. The Committee has now taken this up. The Minister of Economic Affairs and Family has supported this. We must expressly stress this on this point. ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00215_d7e12217	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It has been negotiated for months between the construction social partners on this entire package for the BUAG-Novelle. And after really difficult negotiations, it has succeeded in agreeing on an overall package which contains many points that redefine the regulations between workers and employers in the field of the Civilian Vacation and Processing Act. Since this is said to be a deterioration, I would like to make a few points clear. We are the first industry to succeed in this innovation that, in the event of the death of an employee, the compensation is 100 percent, regardless of how the incomes are in the family. We have also agreed that the BUAK will in future report the working hours of part-time workers on construction. It is hard to believe that of 130 000 workers in the construction industry, 7 500 were part-time on construction sites in the previous year. Because of this circumvention of full-time work by part-time, where control bodies do not know when someone in part-time, when he is in full-time, we agreed that the working hours of part-time workers must be reported on the construction and can therefore also control the control bodies. The resolution charge for the BUAG operations is not applicable. In return, however, there will be a replacement service for the labour market administration by the building workers' holiday and handling fund. The fourth point: Expiration periods are adapted to the general holiday law. It is true, Mrs Belakovich, that means three years. If a person does not pay his leave after three years, the loss is given, as is the case with all other employees, although the holiday fund informs workers in advance: Attention, decay! Get your money! The fifth point: We have also agreed that old leave is to be consumed before unemployment. All other employees in Austria, when a service relationship is terminated, have to consume the old leave or leave the holiday, and only then do they receive unemployment benefit. In the construction industry we have a special arrangement: Two holidays he takes with him, and the last old leave he must consume when the employer cancels, because he thus shortens unemployment and thus increases his annual income. The “greatest child” is the bridging model. The construction social partnership is the first sector in Austria in which employers and employees say: Our people work hard, we don't push them off to the state, we don't put them into a disability pension, into a health insurance scheme or into an unemployment allowance, but for the period missing a heavy worker, a construction worker, we take it – from 58 to 60. Year of life, when he reached 43 years of insurance in the final effect – in a factual service relationship with the BUAK. And if he has his 45 years of insurance, he goes seamlessly to the heavy work pension, exactly the pension that every heavy worker in Austria deserves. This is the first sector, ladies and gentlemen, in which people are not forced into a pension in advance, but with this bridge money, the workers are taken over, so that the retirement pension takes place up to two years later, also for a construction worker. I would like to thank all those who have worked on this solution. It was laborious. It was hard. I thank you, especially in the construction industry, represented by the Bundesinnungsmeister Hans-Werner Frömmel and the speaker of the construction industry Hans Peter Haselsteiner, who succeeded in carrying out the negotiations in many months. But especially thanks to all those responsible in the Social Ministry, our Federal Minister Rudolf Hundstorfer and his team, who really worked hard,! I would also like to thank the coalition partner, who, after having succeeded in convincing him of this model, has ultimately contributed to it, and to all those political parties here in the National Council, who will now give their approval. Thank you very much on behalf of all the heavy workers, construction workers in Austria! - Thank you.

Neubauer: Have just made you!]] It is incorrect that the saving of regulatory capitalisation is directly equivalent to a saving of costs from losses and thus a lower burden on taxpayers. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The path of capitalization that we have taken, namely recapitalisation – for example last year through a bond where the federal government took over the liability – was always cheaper than these entire assets to transfer to a bad bank and to put them around the taxpayer. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Belakovich-Jenewein.]] Since the emergency stateization, we have been consistently working on a solution that will bring forward both the bank's problems and the taxpayers' burden. A quick shot is not appropriate. Bad Banks do not generate economic added value, and I am very glad that Mr Kogler in ZiB 2 has said loud and clear that there is no guarantee that a Bad Bank would have been the cheaper variant. [[Abg. Wheeler: Look!]] – That is why, Mr Kogler, you are not convinced of your model yourself, but you only want to organize election-fights here today. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is also repeatedly mentioned that a Bad Bank would have fewer capital requirements. To this end, it must be said that long-term economic capital needs are largely identical and only regulatory capital needs are reduced. A Bad Bank must also be adequately fed with capital and liquidity, and the risks arising from the credit portfolio will naturally be borne by the State and the taxpayer. The alleged advantage of a lower equity capital is the loss which results from depreciation immediately in the deficit. In other words, a bad bank would all be more expensive for the taxpayer than a more creative solution that cannot be attributed to the taxpayer. [[Congratulations and bravoruf at the ÖVP.]] A long-term approach must be well thought out. There are several intelligent ways that public debt would not suddenly raise. This would be the case, for example, if the state does not hold the majority of a collecting society, but other private institutions. The Bad Bank enthusiasts are only too happy to forget that the transfer of problematic assets to a Bad Bank will result in significant losses plus an increased deficit and an increase in government debt for taxpayers. As long as the aid procedure is not yet completed, the decision-making base is missing, and I would like to avoid the intentionless shots in the air. The Commission now has the restructuring plan. It is only on the basis of the Commission decision that further action is needed and possible. Different variants are analysed and calculated with the involvement of the Bank and the Task Force. As soon as the Commission decision is available, it can and will implement the best possible variant quickly. Questions 13 and 15: In the Federal Finance Act 2013, €46 700 million of capital subsidies are planned for the Hypo Alpe-Adria in the UG. As there are sufficient reserves available in the UG 46, no amendment of the BFG and the BFRG would be necessary. Question 14: For the most part, €133 million, which has been set up as disbursements in the UG 46, is € 128 million, the dedicated payments from the special contribution to the stability levy. You may no longer remember that the aid for the stability levy is intended for restructuring banks and has therefore not been counted among the percentages of income of the countries. In a wise perspective, we have designed that very thing. The remaining €5 million is other payments from the operating activity. On the question 16: ==References== On June 15, 2013 there is a total liability for Hypo Alpe-Adria-Bank International AG of €15.8 billion. Of this, €1.8 billion is the liability of the federal government and € 14 billion is the liability of the country of Carinthia. Liabilities are reduced due to the time-limits of the underlying capital market instruments. The assumed liabilities after IBSG amounting to €0.6 billion will expire in July 2013. The guarantees given in December 2012 for a subordinate bond of the Bank under FinStaLaw amounting to € 1 billion expire in December 2022. Carinthia's national liability is constantly reduced. In 2008, the national liabilities amounted to €22.2 billion, and in recent years they have been reduced to around €14 billion. Questions 17 and 18: The Republic of Austria took place on Saturday, 29. In June 2013, the European Commission sent the new restructuring plan to Hypo Alpe-Adria. The main contents of the restructuring plan are: The Austrian bank HBA is to be sold by the end of 2013. This could be done after the purchase agreement with a British-Indian investor on 31 December. It has already been reached in May. ==References====External links== No new business will be carried out and dismantled on July 2013. The South East European banks, the entire SEA network, will be sold by mid-2015. In the interests of all Austrian taxpayers, the time limit set by the Commission in March for the sale of all marketable units by the end of this year, which would have led to a billion-dollar reduction in value for the Republic. The time limit for selling the South-Eastern Europe banks by the middle of 2015 will allow the Board of Management of Hypo Alpe-Adria to put the sale process in a reasonable time frame and thereby achieve a loss-reducing price. The restructuring plan provides that those parts which cannot be sold must be arranged and reduced in order of value. We have no time-limit for this, so we can find creative solutions here. How the degradation part is organized and legally established was not to be defined in the restructuring plan, but will be decided between the Republic as the owner and the bank. On questions 22 and 25: The organs of the Hypo Alpe-Adria were fully integrated into the development of the rehabilitation concept that is reflected in the restoration plan. Since the beginning of 2013, many discussions have been held with the European Commission, in which representatives of the Bank have almost always participated. These discussions took place partly at the technical level in Brussels and in Vienna, partly within the framework of telephone conferences and also personally with Vice-President Almunia. I myself met Commissioner Almunia for the first time in August 2011 – that was three and a half months after I became Minister of Finance – then again and again at various ECOFIN Councils. I've got 5 on an appointment. On 25 April, he discussed the restructuring plan with him in Brussels for a longer period of time and then with him on 25 April. In June here in Vienna, the cornerstones of the plan are finalized. It is therefore wrong, Mr Kogler, if you said in your introductory remarks that I would not have taken care of this causa. The involvement of the Bank's institutions was always given because, due to the data content of the restructuring documents, they must be drawn up by the Executive Board and coordinated with the Supervisory Board within the bank. The work of the Task Force, which has now been set up, has also been carried out several times in the banking sector. The bank institutions, both in terms of the Supervisory Board and discussions with Director-General Kranebitter, also took part in discussions in the department. Questions 23 and 24: It was examined in advance by the Bank whether the BayernLB had certain consent rights in the further development of the bank's company law. The legal options depend on the interpretation of the respective agreements, on the further design of the bank and thus on the intended restructuring concept. Neither when a collecting or settlement company is established nor when the Hypo Alpe-Adria-Bank International's shares are sold, the BayernLB as a former owner also has rights beyond that. The further design and handling of possible consent rights are solely the institutions of the Bank, which will also act in the interests of the owner. In the Federal Ministry of Finance, no advisory costs have been incurred for the Hypo Alpe-Adria since the emergency stateization, as in the sense of economy and economy, only domestic know-how has been used. Legal advice has been given by the Financial Procuratorship. External consultants commissioned in individual cases, e.g. for the examination of recovery plans, were to be paid by the bank on the basis of the agreements made in the course of support measures. [[Abg. Strache: Under what title has this been charged if there were no consulting costs? Under what title?]] Under my authority, it has also been found that the money of the Landesbank Bayern, which the Bayern still have in the mortgage, has capital quality, and is therefore prohibited by the legal basis in accordance with the equity replacement law that this money is repaid to Bayern. Bayern sees this differently and has filed a complaint to determine the quality of this capital. This process is pending and I do not want to comment more in detail on pending procedures. Thank you.

Mr Ostermayer, State Secretary, rightly cited two recent reports, by OECD and the EU Commission. We have recently also had a third position, which is usually very critical – it was with us two and a half weeks ago by the International Monetary Fund, whose final report we now also have as a preprint – and which is quite the same tenor: Be proud of what you have done. You're on your way! You've had a great crisis management! You have a very good grip on budget consolidation! . So all the reports here cover entirely, those of OECD, Monetary Fund and EU. On the other hand, of course, the challenges are also rightly mentioned, and I must honestly say that this is actually a sort of latent for the next government, because undoubtedly we have enormous challenges, even though we are proud that all these three organisations recognise that our economic and financial policy, including social security, is actually based on this three-pillar principle, namely budget consolidation, second structural reforms and third impulses for growth and employment. This has led to the key figures mentioned by State Secretary Ostermayer, namely: higher growth compared to the average of the euro-states, better employment situation, second place in the population income statistics per capita. This is already a success of this very smart, far-sighted, sustainable economic policy strategy of this Federal Government. But, once again, I am also very glad that the challenges facing the future have been highlighted, and I would like to take out only two areas. On the one hand: All of Europe is looking forward to growth, and we are constantly saying that we need more growth. But now all three reports say quite equally that we have two growth brakes in our country. One is the access of many small and medium-sized enterprises to finance, and the second is an increasing shortage of labour. Many businesses say – we had a meeting again yesterday in the field of crowd-funding, since we will take a first small step tomorrow – it is so difficult for us to come to finance. And others say we have too few qualified workers. – These are two growth brakes. We have to eliminate these, and we also have higher economic growth. And that. Second, of course, are the long-term challenges. We have recently – you know – the long-term budget forecast that demographic expenditure will rise from 31.5 percent to 34.5 percent by 2050. That's a huge thing. These are 10 billion € per year after today's money; in 2050 it will probably be much more. I have to say honestly, I always say the big ticking time bomb in our state budget are the pensions. However, the political message must not be: Yours Must work longer!, but the message must be: We politicians will do everything so you can work longer! – These are challenges to health policy, to skills policy, by the way, also to the social partners’ wage policy – approximation of life income curve and life performance curve. There are really enormous challenges, and I must say now that I will no longer be in Parliament during the new legislative period: I already wish the future government, the future majority of Parliament, much success in meeting these challenges!

When I speak of improvements, I have already mentioned one, namely the possibility of getting to Austrian citizenship faster. On the other hand, married and unmarried children are equated with citizenship law. If at least a parent of Austrian citizens is, then the principle of descent applies, regardless of whether the parents are married or not. The naturalisation of adoptive children is facilitated and accelerated. Moreover – and I have already explained this – it will enable disabled people who are not able or only partially able to pursue an acquisition and thus finance their lives to also gain citizenship. Children up to 14 years of age also have easier access to Austrian citizenship. So many measures have been taken to speed up Austrian citizenship, I give you the right, Mr Vilimsky, but they are for children. Unmarried and married children are treated as equal. On the other hand, I also said that we also have various restrictive measures which have not changed. I already believe that these restrictive measures are a guarantee that a step towards adapting to our society system will be taken. We should not take out individual situations, but see the situation in general. I believe that with this novel, we are on a very, very good way to better integrate people and allow them to gain Austrian citizenship more quickly.

Well known in this context, your Green Council has come with the One-Way ticket and said: Actually, all of them are supposed to go home with a one-way ticket to Turkey! The next day he had to correct this and apologize for it. – The whole story has also led your group a little into instability, so that is no longer quite understandable. I'll take you, however. Your commitment, Mr State Secretary, to integration, will be complete. It is indeed sad that we have not processed the backpack, which we have been carrying with us since the 1990s after the main wave of immigration in the nearer history of Austria, that there are still people with us who came to Austria in the 1990s – for whatever reason – and have not yet been integrated into the country. They want to do this now and have done quite reasonable projects, for example the project with the integration ambassadors "Together: Austria’. These are very good projects I would like to fully support. On the other hand, the successes are still waiting for them. One knows that integration is a process that must and may even go for years or decades, but this Erdogan demo has been dissuasive, because it has nothing to do with Austria's identification, which for me must be a fundamental feature of citizenship. It's kind of remarkable and shows that integration still doesn't work if there are demonstrators who are Austrian citizens saying: With the head and with the spirit I am actually Turks and not Austrian at all! Why am I saying this in a debate on citizenship? I have already said this in all the appreciation of your work in this field in the Committee: For me, the citizenship award is not a motive for integration. Mr. Col. von der ÖVP, I do not think that the citizenship award – whether it takes place sooner or later – is a motive for integration. I don't think so. If someone comes here and – let us assume this once – is actually integrative, then it is not his first concern to become a citizen. His first concern is to get a firm stay, get a job, feed himself and, if necessary, his family, learn the German language and be able to integrate as fully as possible. And I believe that only at the end of the chain is the desire to acquire citizenship or early citizenship. I am going to conform to this point with the colleague of the SPÖ. I don't think there's going to be a run on this premature citizenship either. And if we find that out, then again the question arises: What does that make sense? Why do we need to create a few criteria that allow early citizenship, which is not so much desired? I am glad that – I have also said this in committee – the general conditions that are relatively sharp and that sometimes come from a time when we have been responsible for government continue to be upright. So: Insolence, secure living, German knowledge, and also two points that are not unimportant, namely a positive attitude to the Republic of Austria and no close relationship with extremist groups. These are two very important points for me, which in one case or another – see demonstration – are very likely to be considered. So: Citizenship is certainly not a motive for integration. There won't be the big run. Therefore: So what? What do we need such a reduction? I would have done it the other way round, Mr State Secretary. I would have said: If certain conditions – better knowledge of German, dispute of one's own livelihood in Austria – are met, someone gets a citizenship on trial. I see, in contrast to you, the previous prospect not already as a sample, because I think you can only have something on trial, which you already have and not something you don't have. – So I would have introduced these rules: After six years citizenship will be awarded on trial for four years, and in these four years the new citizen must prove himself. And if he is proven and nothing happens, he has the right to permanent citizenship after ten years – as is the case now. However, in view of the demonstrations and so many attitudes that exist, I would have introduced an element, namely the possibility to deny citizenship for displaced people. – I believe it It must also be possible that if someone suddenly fails to meet one of the basic criteria for citizenship – if he becomes criminal, if he no longer confesses to the Republic, if he suddenly believes that he wants to join an extremist Islamic group et cetera – he asks the question: Do you even want to remain an Austrian citizen? I therefore believe that citizenship is on trial and recognition of two fundamental points. Citizenship on and on itself is an elementary civil right with rights and obligations. You shouldn't play with that. I do not see this as a game either, but I see it as a step which, in your opinion, contributes to integration. – I believe that the step is unnecessary and will not make any significant progress in integration, and we therefore reject it.

But I can calm you down at the same time: With the new law, we do not create a slight sale of Austrian citizenship, but quite the contrary: Austrian citizenship continues to be subject to very clear criteria, and in the international comparison it remains a relatively difficult citizenship to achieve, which is justified in my view, because it is a good thing. To you, Mrs Korun: They said there was a lot of need for improvement in the old law. I would like to invite you to see the positive pages, perhaps. We have created an improvement for people with disabilities. We have created an improvement for PutativAustrians. We have created equality between immoral children. We have introduced that citizenship must be granted within a solemn framework. That too, as I believe, is a sensible step, because it is probably not enough to invite someone to become a citizen, and then to forgive citizenship in a bureaucratic act, as if it were not a fixed value and as if that were something to be generated for. However, we have also created a paradigm change concerning the general award of citizenship. We have turned our attention to the length of our stay towards the incentive system. And I am perfectly with you, Mr Westenthaler, that this will not trigger a run on Austrian citizenship. But it clearly shows what we expect from a immigrant. They said that the citizenship award was not a motive for integration. – It may be! But the citizenship award gives us the opportunity to show what we expect from a immigrant. An incentive system gives us the opportunity to show that it is not only a matter of someone taking up their stay in Austria, but also of meeting certain criteria in order to become a citizen. That is why I think it makes sense for us to have a three-stage system: after six years citizenship for all who are very, very well integrated; after ten years citizenship for all those who meet the criteria; and citizenship is to be given no one who cannot or is not self-sustainable or does not exist the citizenship test. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Bitch.]] Mr Westenthaler! They have also said that it would be useful to introduce citizenship on trial. [[Abg. Street: Gift article Citizenship!]] I believe that the six years of waiting for citizenship or the ten years of waiting for citizenship in the normal period of time is something like a parole phase, which is something like a trial period where someone in Austria proves himself to be able to become a citizen when he meets all the criteria. I am glad that we can decide on this law today, can thank all the employees of the BMI for their work, thank me at the coalition partner for the negotiations and for the proposals for improvement that have come and that we have been able to work on. I can also thank the Stronach team for their approval. I am pleased if we can further develop citizenship today. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00215_d7e24529	Madam President. Mr. Minister! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen in the House! ==References====External links== In January of this year, the population survey took place and decided on the 60 percent of the population per military service and civil service. I am glad that we have succeeded in bringing about a reform of the military service, which, on the one hand, brings more security and strengthens both the Federal Army and the civil protection, and on the other hand, enables young men to experience time in the presence service as a useful time and thus take added value into their lives. Secondly, we have succeeded in bringing about a civil service reform which can in any case be seen. We know that, especially in the field of civil service, it is commonplace – both in civil servants and in civil society. There is already a satisfaction of more than 90 percent. Nevertheless, it was important for us to turn on the various screws and make improvements. What are the cornerstones of the improvements? – For the first time, it is the training package. As you know, there is a very comprehensive training for civil servants who actively participate in rescue organizations. We have set ourselves the goal of creating training opportunities in other areas – in the health field, in the elderly and in the disabled. This is also a very good decision, which is very forward-looking, because we all know about demographic development and we have quite clear figures on the table that we need a high number of nurses. Therefore, if we invest in this area, we also assume that we will succeed in keeping many civil servants in the elderly and in the disabled. This is why it is a good investment, for which the funds are spent half by the Ministry of Social Affairs and half by the Ministry of the Interior – a financial support for training of 70 percent of the costs, up to €1 700, which is simply well arranged. Secondly, it is especially important for us to be able to use civil servants according to their competence, their abilities. This means that in the future it will also be possible to introduce professional competence. I am thinking, in particular, of doctors who do not have to provide relief services, but can also be used in the medical service. This brings benefits to all – for civil servants, for the civil service organisations and, of course, for those who are cared for. Thirdly, it was also important for us to reduce bureaucracy and, above all, to ensure flexibility in the allocation. So far, an assignment could be made up to two weeks before the arrival date. In the future, there will be a three-day period when the agreement of the civil servant is available. This gives more flexibility and more space, especially for the operational organisations. Fourthly, it is important for us to be able to count primarily on the skills and competences acquired during civil service. Therefore, at the end of the civil service, there will be a so-called competence balance, which clearly explains the skills and knowledge acquired. The trainings and the activities carried out during the civil service are mentioned. How this balance of expertise will be looked out in detail will be set out by a regulation. Another innovation, with which I also thank Mr Labour and Social Minister, is that it will be possible to offer civil servants a study and professional advice, with the AMS in particular being an important hub. It is also important to us that civil servants have the opportunity to assess the institution in which they operate. Why is that important to us? – Because the quality ensures and above all ensures a good handling of staff with civil servants. Another innovation, which has been mentioned briefly, which is not directly related to civil service, is the voluntary social year. We make it possible for those young men who complete the social service within the framework of the voluntary social year to be counted as civil service. Thus, those who make this voluntary social year are also equated with those who are engaged in foreign service. It is also very important that the voluntary year be opened for rescue organizations. Who will benefit above all? – In particular, the women for whom the possibility of getting into this area is created, of course, as an alternative to civil service for women. Ladies and gentlemen, this revision will be decided today. From tomorrow it will be fully implemented. I would like to take the opportunity to thank all the experts, all the organisations involved, the two ministries – the Social Ministry and the Interior – and all the staff involved. Thank you too, Mr Minister, Mr Rudi, for this really good cooperation. We can say that once again we have succeeded in making civil service even a little better. - Thank you.

On average, these are 100 children per year who are affected here, and therefore essentially we always see the high-quality protection interest of children in the foreground, and everything else is secondary. Whether there could be data protection problems now, whether it is too little to inform the headmaster only, this can be discussed, but the focus is on the high-quality interest of protecting children. This is guaranteed here, and that is why we can vote for it out of full conviction. Following this exemplary implementation in the Security Police Act, I would like to see similar measures with a stronger protection of children also in the Criminal Code. I know, Mrs Minister, that you are not directly affected here, but we have always said this from this point of view: If we define the child as a particularly protective person and children as a particularly protective group – and we do so today with the improvement in the Security Police Act – then this must finally also be found in the Criminal Code. And then the criminal code must no longer distinguish between sexual abuse and severe sexual abuse in the case of sexual abuse of children. This must not be distinguished because any abuse of children is difficult. And we demand that this is finally reflected in the criminal code. Otherwise, we will find that good today and will agree with it.

Point Report of the Committee on Internal Affairs on the Government Template (2132, e.g._Other Organiser Protocol against the illicit manufacture of firearms, associated parts and components and ammunition, and against illicit trade, in addition to the United Nations Convention against Transboundary Organised Crime (2552 D.B.)
ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00215_d7e26108	We're going to 30. Agenda item. I would like to draw attention to the fact that there are only 11 minutes of speaking time. ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-04-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00215_d7e26142	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! Fighting international organised crime and terrorism remains one of the major challenges of our time. The share of crimes involving international references and the proportion of cases involving suspects of different nationalities are increasing. According to the United Nations estimates, several hundred billion US dollars are being implemented annually by transnational organised criminals. The fields of activity range from drug trafficking to money laundering, but also from human trafficking and arms trafficking. An effective fight against this form of crime is therefore only possible through international cooperation and at international level. An important step towards increased cooperation is the one taken by the General Assembly of the United Nations Convention against Transboundary Organised Crime, supplemented and subsequently expanded by the Additional Protocols against Migrants and Trafficking in Human Beings. We are dealing today with the third Additional Protocol, which deals with the illicit manufacture of firearms, their parts and components and ammunition, as well as the illicit trade. The fight against illegal production and trafficking in firearms must be fought by all means, because only in this way can terrorism on the one hand and organised crime on the other and the withdrawal of these resources. Overall, these four international treaties create a highly complex framework for police and judicial cooperation. But above all, it is about the signal effect, because the Treaties make it clear our determination to take up the fight with organised crime worldwide.

Prerequisite must be: Before a student’s generous and quality development, the need must also be ensured, and not vice versa. I know from some communities – and I know a community very well – that it has been extended generously and in quality. Only after that has been raised whether or not the need has been met, and that has not been the case. That's how years have passed. So vice versa would actually be the right way. For us, freedom of choice is also important, which must also be in the foreground, regardless of the full day school form, whether with a pure afternoon care after class or also limited day schools in which lessons and leisure alternate. Finally, I would like to address the appreciation of parents, namely those parents who do not use the whole day’s school form and care for their children in the afternoon, because exactly with those parents, the state saves itself a lot of money. Thank you.

These loathsome acts of the former Czechoslovakian, the former Yugoslavs, the former USSR did not happen in the course of a war, but at times of peace. That is why you really have to work up the story and teach the children, because what happened to those displaced, the people of the Germans, has happened under the eyes of the Allies, under the eyes of the French, British and Americans looking away or watching. This theme must be equally illuminated. Ladies and gentlemen! This is probably the beginning of the falsification of history that this is not taught to our children. This is probably the case, because as a young Member you may not have learned what actually happened at school. Or – what I don't want to accept – you don't want to see the facts come true. It is precisely what our ancestors experienced and suffered there that needs to be prepared, especially since we know that they have worked very well in the construction of the Second Republic, in the construction of Austria after the war. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Austria would not have experienced this progress if the people of Germany and displaced persons, who had been stateless for a long time, had not cooperated. Everyone knew about it, even you, my dear displaced speakers, if you had only visited one of our events. There you would have learned if you had heard what the displaced women and young people suffered for fate. I would therefore like to make the following motion for a resolution by Mr Kitzmüller and other Members: The National Council wants to decide: “The Federal Minister for Education, Arts and Culture is invited to focus on political education at the end of World War II 70 years ago, starting with the 2014/15 school year. The consequences and impact on the political system in Europe and Austria as well as the European Union should be adequately discussed. Using the possibilities of the framework curriculum, topics such as the Holocaust and other genocides, totalitarian ideologies, deportations, in particular the AltAustrian, crimes of the TITO parties in Carinthia and Styria, the liquidation of Croatian armed forces and civilians in the Bleiburg region, the East-West conflict, alliance systems and international organizations and the development of democracies with reference to current world-political events are to be discussed and addressed. ==References====External links== Insert “Oral History” into the curriculum and provide sufficient financial resources for the teaching aids.”
